# Fixed issues

Archive of rows retired from `scratch/known_issues.md`. A row moves here only
when the fix is merged; the SHA stays with it. Kept so a later lane can see what
shape these defects took — several were subsystems that compiled, tested and
shipped with nothing calling them.

Older tables use the pre-D473 5-column shape: `Status | Sev | Issue | Evidence |
Owner`. The live ledger gained a `Class` column in D473; archived rows keep the
shape they were retired in; post-D473 imports retain the `Class` column.

I/O APIC vector routing and GIC INTID masking shut down the physical source; built-in UART and keyboard handlers report whether they claimed it. Exact-boundary and deferred-thread positive controls, 408 focused tests, both full-feature architecture builds, and paired smoke passed. | B1870-irq-storm-shutdown |

| FIXED 2014f7eaf | blocker | The x86_64 16550 runtime console held its port lock and busy-polled `LSR.THRE` before synchronously writing every byte, charging the caller for KVM port-I/O exits instead of letting the UART interrupt make progress. | Runtime writes now copy into an allocation-free 64 KiB transmit ring, arm `IER.THRI` only on the disabled-to-enabled transition, drain at most the 16-byte hardware FIFO per interrupt after servicing RX, and clear THRI when empty. | FIXED ac48bbb79 | high | Early device IRQs could nest without bound on the per-CPU hard-IRQ stack because x86 acknowledged a level source before its handler and sampled bottom-half nesting after reopening interrupts. | Acknowledgement now follows handling and the bottom-half count is captured before interrupts reopen. | FIXED 971f92d04 | med | The `nscg` hosted tests installed one process-global namespace callback from multiple test-module call sites. | FIXED 333b1faa8 | med | Paired smoke boots raced while populating one worktree's missing ARM GRUB vendor tree, so one process could remove modules while the other extracted them. | ARM vendor preflight is serialized per worktree and validates the required module set after acquiring the lock. | FIXED db7e0d4cf | low | The serial TTY echo regression expected bare LF even though Linux's default `OPOST|ONLCR` output policy correctly expands LF to CRLF. | Both stale assertions now expect CRLF; the full workspace suite and both architecture gates pass. | B1845-devpts-per-mount-instances |
| FIXED F828 | high | The IRQ stack gate counted exit-to-user work as hardirq-stack usage even though both architecture stubs restore the interrupted task stack before calling it. | FIXED C244 | high | Routine gate set compiled NO feature-gated code, so a branch that does not build could pass every check — the failure then surfaced as an EMPTY `make qemu-*` log, which reads like a boot failure rather than a build one. `make feature-gate` existed but nothing invoked it routinely; `.githooks/pre-push` returned early on every PR-branch push and only ever ran boot-smoke. | Fixed by `xtask kernel --check` (type-check only: no codegen, link, ELF snapshot or rootfs) behind `make feature-gate`, wired into `pre-push` for EVERY push touching `kernel/ crates/ userspace/ targets/ vendor/ tools/xtask/ Cargo.*`. Positive control (E0308 injected in a `debug_boot!` block in `kmain/runtime.rs`): default-feature check GREEN 0 errors, `make feature-gate` RED `error[E0308]: mismatched types --> crates/kernel/kmain/src/kmain/runtime.rs:137:29` / `make: *** [feature-gate-x86] Error 101`; error removed -> GREEN both arches. Cost: 43 s cold in a fresh worktree (22 s x86 + 20 s aarch64), 5 s warm. | C244 |

## Keyring

| Status | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| FIXED B1865 | high | Six live `request_key(2)` rows still claimed that the images lacked keyutils, no real construction completed, the helper's fork/exec credential hooks were unproved, the nested-helper pool case had no boot proof, and aarch64 had never run it. Those were duplicate, stale descriptions of the B1672/B1704 state; the unique implementation defects and their fixes already live below. | All four current lite/gnome × x86_64/aarch64 images contain the distribution `/sbin/request-key`. On current `main` (`e7631f1af`), canonical `make -j2 smoke-request-key` passed first attempt on x86_64 and aarch64 using the exact `REQUEST-KEY-PROBE: PASS` marker. The probe requires both the ordinary construction payload and a nested construction payload carrying the inner key serial, so success traverses the real helper, handler exec/fork, token inheritance, answer caching, and concurrent UMH pool growth. Linux reference inspection confirms `prepare_creds` retains `request_key_auth`, process-keyring search follows the token to the original requester, and `UMH_WAIT_PROC` work runs on concurrency-capable unbound workers. | B1865-request-key-live-proof-fold |
| FIXED 81667bf9e | low | `try_fill_iter` carried a seemingly redundant `total <= cap` term in its atomic-write guard. | The capacity floor is now explicitly tested as at least `PIPE_BUF`; therefore `total <= PIPE_BUF` already implies `total <= cap`. The guard is correct and no behavior change was needed. Focused pipe invariant regression and full pre-push gates passed. | D497-pipe-atomicity-guard-ledger |
| FIXED 5a9cf0965 | low | A closed notification pipe left its key watches attached, so subsequent key events posted into a queue nobody could read. | Each queue now tracks its reverse key-watch membership; pipe teardown removes those exact watches without posting a removal record into the dead pipe. The registry teardown regression and `cargo test -p fs --lib` (938 tests) passed, along with the full pre-push gates. | D495-key-watch-queue-close-cleanup-ledger |
| FIXED a3d215fc4 | low | The keyring UAPI header incorrectly described capability bits as unimplemented and unread. | The header now separates intentionally unreferenced UAPI ids from capability bits, which are computed by the implementing modules. Focused DH, public-key, and watch capability regressions pin the contract. | D493-keyring-capabilities-ledger |
| FIXED B1666 | high | B1579's recorded hazard — "a usermode helper started concurrently with early userspace on SMP=2 wedges (kernel-context VFS lookup vs a concurrent user lookup)" — does NOT reproduce, and the real user-reachable defect on that path was a different one: ALL helpers were serviced by a SINGLE thread that, under `UMH_WAIT_PROC`, blocks for the whole lifetime of the helper program. That is head-of-line blocking for every other pending helper, and a permanent DEADLOCK for a helper that itself asks the kernel for a helper — which `/sbin/request-key` does by design (the construction default names the ORIGINAL requester's keyring precisely so a nested request works). The nested request could only be served by the thread already waiting for the program that issued it, and the requester's wait is uninterruptible. | B1666. NEGATIVE result on the recorded wedge: x86_64 `smp=2` KVM boot with `debug-umh`, all five self-test probes correct while systemd ran concurrently — `absent-wait-exec rc=-2`, `notaprog-wait-exec rc=-13`, `present-wait-exec rc=0`, `present-wait-proc rc=0`, `absent-wait-proc rc=-2` at t=92.0-93.3 s, boot continued to a GNOME session at t=202 s. (The mechanism B1579 described — a loader resuming on another process's page tables after a sleep — was itself fixed inside B1579 by borrowing the address space rather than switching only the page-table root; the row outlived its cause.) FIX: `umh::pool` sizes the servicing contexts on demand — the thread that takes a request starts a successor first, capped at the queue depth, so nothing that fits on the queue waits behind another request rather than behind its own helper. Hosted `tests/pool.rs` 8 tests (`umh` 28 -> 36 passed / 0 failed); both arches type-check with and without `debug-umh`. | B1666 |
| FIXED 579d405bd | high | `inherit_session` had NO caller outside its own test. No forked child ever inherited a session keyring; nothing purged a dead task's entries, so a RECYCLED tid inherited the previous occupant's keys, and every task touching `@s`/`@t`/`@p` leaked a keyring plus its quota charge permanently. | B1649. Now `lifecycle::{fork,exec,exit,fsids_changed}` wired at clone/execve/exit/commit_creds; `exit_purges_the_tid_and_frees_the_keyring`, `exit_refunds_the_quota_charge`. | B1649 |
| FIXED 244c9e5f6 | high | Between minting an authorisation token and handing the helper the keyring holding it, NEITHER was reachable from any gc root. Any concurrent `collect()` — an unrelated task unlinking a key is enough — destroyed both and stranded the requester on a key no helper could then answer. A real intermittent production failure, surfaced only because the hosted tests run in parallel against the global store. | B1649. `Store::collect` now roots live tokens and the keyring linking them. | B1649 |
| FIXED 244c9e5f6 | high | `KEYCTL_GET_PERSISTENT` aliased the USER keyring — wrong owner, wrong lifetime (the user keyring dies with the last session; the persistent one must outlive logout), and gated on `CAP_SYS_ADMIN` where the reference uses `CAP_SETUID`. | B1649. Now a real `_persistent.<uid>` in a `.persistent_register`, destination mandatory, expiry refreshed per use; `the_persistent_keyring_is_not_the_user_keyring`. | B1649 |
| FIXED 244c9e5f6 | high | `KEYCTL_SEARCH` flattened every failure to ENOKEY, discarding WHY the search failed. That both hid EACCES/EKEYREVOKED/EKEYEXPIRED from callers and made negative-key caching impossible, so an unresolvable name would re-run the helper on every request. Three pre-existing tests asserted the flattened behaviour and encoded the wrong belief. | B1649. `ops/search.rs` now reproduces the skip-reason propagation and the `success > ENOKEY > EAGAIN > other` merge. | B1649 |
| FIXED 244c9e5f6 | med | `request_key` did not distinguish `callout == NULL` from `callout == ""`; both suppressed construction. The empty string must upcall. | B1649, `an_empty_callout_string_still_upcalls`. | B1649 |
| FIXED 244c9e5f6 | med | `KEYCTL_REVOKE` did not retry with `KEY_NEED_SETATTR` on EACCES, so a key whose mask grants Setattr but not Write could not be withdrawn by its holder; and it used a partial lookup, so a second revoke reported EACCES instead of EKEYREVOKED. | B1649, `revoke_falls_back_to_setattr_permission`, `revoking_twice_reports_the_key_is_already_revoked`. | B1649 |
| FIXED 244c9e5f6 | med | `KEYCTL_SET_TIMEOUT` and `KEYCTL_GET_SECURITY` had no authorisation-token path, so a helper could not bound or inspect the key it was asked to build. `KEYCTL_JOIN_SESSION_KEYRING` accepted a `.`-prefixed name, which would place a caller inside `.persistent_register`. | B1649, `set_timeout_accepts_the_authorisation_token_instead_of_setattr`, `a_dot_prefixed_session_name_is_refused`. | B1649 |
| FIXED 68f197dc4 | med | `/proc/keys` and `/proc/key-users` were empty static stubs and `/proc/sys/kernel/keys/*` did not exist at all. | B1649. Both live and per-reader filtered; four ceilings plus `persistent_keyring_expiry` bound to the live values `key_alloc` and `KEYCTL_GET_PERSISTENT` consult. | B1649 |

## Drivers / devices

| Status | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| FIXED B1875 | blocker | **Physical hardware had no framebuffer source: the complete VT/fbcon/fbdev stack could bind only to virtio-gpu, while the Multiboot2 handoff neither requested nor retained a firmware scanout.** | The x86 header now requests a loader-selected mode, the tag-8 parser validates and preserves exact packed-RGB geometry in `BootInfo`, and `drv-simplefb` registers a page-offset-aware, driver-owned WC mapping only after PCI probing finds no native fbdev. The fbdev mode retains firmware pitch, depth, and channel masks; damage copies convert non-XRGB formats. Hosted parser/format/damage tests and both release builds pass. A forced QEMU std-VGA boot with no virtio-gpu registered the 1280x800 framebuffer and reached userspace. Its 125 MiB repeated full-frame write took 0.27 s with WC versus 3.10 s under a temporary UC control; the control was restored and never committed. Full measurements are retained in `scratch/simplefb-performance-20260806.md`. | B1875-physical-framebuffer-source |
| FIXED B1875 | low | **Growing `BootInfo` with the framebuffer handoff deepened the already-ratcheted x86 boot chain by 16 bytes, so the stack gate rejected `_start` at 20,016 bytes.** | Both architecture boot builders now construct the complete handoff in boot-owned static storage and pass a shared reference into `kernel_main`, instead of returning the enlarged structure through `_start_rust`'s stack. The x86 chain fell to 19,904 bytes (96 bytes below its prior baseline), and the aarch64 chain remains below its 13 KiB ceiling at 12,129 bytes. Both stack gates pass. | B1875-physical-framebuffer-source |
| FIXED 18936f7b5, a5a9a5b0d, 667c8a2da, 32b37265d | high | **The real-hardware ledger claimed AP bring-up was a counting stub after both architecture paths had already shipped.** | `kmain` calls the architecture owners, not the unused generic scaffold: x86 copies the real-mode-to-long-mode trampoline below 1 MiB, sends INIT/SIPI, then installs per-CPU GS, GDT/IDT/TSS, syscall and IRQ stacks, LAPIC timer, runqueue, and online state; arm64 performs PSCI CPU_ON and reaches the same scheduler participation. SMP=2 boots and watchdog reports already exercise both CPUs. The stale row and real-hardware section are retired instead of building a second startup path. | B1875-physical-framebuffer-source |
| FIXED 5f78b2e22, fe49e882f | med | **The synchronous virtio-block path needed a 64-probe IRQ-enabled bridge before it could sleep, unlike an ordinary completion wait.** | The driver checks the used index once and parks directly; only explicitly polled I/O may loop. Syscalls run ordinary process work IRQ-enabled and mask before return work; synchronous user faults inherit process IRQ state while atomic faults stay masked. The focused regression and its restored-bridge positive control pass, `drv-virtio-blk` passes 30/30, both release targets build, and three one-vCPU Firefox boots prove the completion IRQ wakes the parked task. Process-context softirq drains use each architecture's IRQ stack, matching Linux and keeping both task-stack gates green. | B1872-virtio-blk-direct-wait |

## Net / socket

| Status | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| FIXED 343f57d43, 884f2f966 | med | **Installed nftables policy was reparsed and cloned from mutable global vectors for every packet.** | Netlink now owns canonical namespace-qualified control state, validates complete rules and set bindings before install, commits a whole batch atomically, and publishes one immutable compiled generation under RCU. Packet evaluation takes no control lock, does no parsing/cloning/allocation, resolves compiled sets by index, and updates retained atomic counters. The 64-rule release benchmark improved from 3,973 to 188 ns/packet median (21.1x); netfilter passed 43 tests with the benchmark retained as an ignored control, netlink passed 262, net passed 2,063, the lint ratchet and 103-crate isolated hosted gate passed, and both architecture smokes passed first attempt. | B1873-nft-compiled-generation |
| FIXED 343f57d43, 884f2f966 | high | **Firefox invalid-host handling could lock the only vCPU while TCP delivery reconstructed a network namespace owner through the numeric global registry.** | Socket and interface state now carry the concrete namespace owner into transport and ingress state; packet paths upgrade weak owner links and cannot enter the numeric registry, including IGMP/MLD receive. Pre-fix run `1540519` stopped in `network_namespace::registry::lookup_u64`; profiled run `1578707` found no registry loop, and exact final run `1909894` rendered the valid page in 4.960 s and invalid-DNS result in 2.700 s with 0.150 us kernel time per syscall and no watchdog, fault, or stall. Full evidence is in `scratch/firefox-performance-20260806.md`. | B1873-nft-compiled-generation |
| FIXED 5f78b2e22, fe49e882f | high | **Firefox page loading could freeze when process-context networking was interrupted by NET_RX while holding shared state under a plain spinlock.** | Shared receive-path state now uses Linux-shaped bottom-half exclusion, including own-IRQ-stack softirq drains. Three cold boots passed the full valid/invalid-DNS harness: `1337758` (4.944 s / 1.465 s), `1338334` (6.095 s / 3.854 s), and post-stack-switch `1435376` (6.105 s / 2.679 s). Kernel syscall cost was 0.153–0.165 us/call and the final HTTPS control was 34.154 ms, with no watchdog, fault, or resolver/control loss. Full evidence is in `scratch/firefox-performance-20260806.md`. | B1872-virtio-blk-direct-wait |
| FIXED b2860a72a | high | Passive TCP child construction held the complete connection and entry as simultaneous stack temporaries on the softirq path. | The child is allocated first and initialized through its owning lock. The size regression, 1,946 net tests, and both smoke arches passed. | B1726-tcp-passive-child-frame |
| FIXED 850971704 | high | `SIOCSIFFLAGS` compared the caller's 16-bit administrative flags against a reported word containing derived carrier flags, so every request was rejected after carrier reporting was corrected. | The ungated decision accepts only caller-writable administrative bits and preserves volatile/derived state. | FIXED b2669b8ad | med | A negotiated `VIRTIO_NET_F_STATUS` change was never consumed after probe, leaving carrier state and `RTM_NEWLINK` stale. | The config interrupt defers status refresh to the network bottom half and emits one notification for a real carrier transition. | FIXED 0e423a637 | high | A carrier-present interface that was administratively down was reported with `IF_OPER_UP`. | Operational state now requires both administrative-up and carrier. The focused netlink suite and serial startup probe passed. | B1760-resolved-scope-startup |
| FIXED 3148521ae | med | AF_VSOCK lacked `SO_ZEROCOPY` / `MSG_ZEROCOPY` and the resulting `MSG_ERRQUEUE` completion cmsg at `SOL_VSOCK` / `VSOCK_RECVERR`. | Connectible sockets now admit and inherit the boolean option, successful requested sends publish coalesced copied-fallback completion ranges and `POLLERR`, and `recvmsg(MSG_ERRQUEUE)` emits the exact 16-byte extended error. | FIXED 646645bce | med | Connected AF_VSOCK `recvmsg` peer-name writeback had no behavioural regression, leaving the syscall wrapper's Linux zero-length-name contract covered only by source inspection. | A full hosted `recv_pinned` test now drives real payload, msghdr, iovec, and name buffers and proves success writes `msg_namelen=0` without touching the name bytes. | FIXED 3dbe4fc730 | med | `recvmsg` had no live host-oracle proof for IPv4 packet info, UNIX credentials, ancillary-destination faults, AF_UNIX urgent data, or IPv4 extended-error payload and metadata. | The portable `t_msg` corpus now executes `IP_PKTINFO`, `SCM_CREDENTIALS`, an invalid control destination, `MSG_OOB`, and loopback `IP_RECVERR` against Linux and booted x86_64/aarch64 kernels; both Oxide frames match the host oracle byte-for-byte. | B1852-recvmsg-ancillary-conformance |
| FIXED 3dbe4fc730 | med | A receive-side ancillary copy fault after payload delivery returned `EFAULT`; Linux's receive producers ignore `put_cmsg` failure, preserve the payload result, and do not advance the control cursor. | The shared receive-control cursor now suppresses that fault without committing failed SCM pidfds. | FIXED 3dbe4fc730 | high | AF_UNIX `recvmsg(MSG_OOB)` entered urgent receive handling while its match scrutinee still held `sock.kind`; peer-name lookup reacquired the same lock and deadlocked the single-vCPU guest, including SSH. | Socket-kind selection now clones the urgent target under the lock and dispatches only after the guard is dropped. | FIXED 3dbe4fc730 | med | A valid unicast UDP datagram to a closed local port was silently discarded, so a connected socket with `IP_RECVERR` received no `ECONNREFUSED` record. | FIXED e7f8199a6 | med | `recvmsg` had no live proof of fd-before-msghdr classification, complete metadata import before dequeue, or payload/name/final-header usercopy ordering; the recvmmsg fake could not exercise any of those target-only paths. | The expanded portable `t_msg` corpus runs those cases against Linux and booted x86_64/aarch64 kernels. | FIXED e7f8199a6 | med | A datagram payload copy that landed a prefix before faulting returned the prefix length as success; Linux returns EFAULT while consuming the record. Every call leaked and no caller received a fragment to reuse. | B1770 now stores the compatible `page`, `offset`, and `size` state, resets exclusive ownership, reuses borrowed capacity, and drops the fragment's reference before replacement. `page_frag_refill_populates_and_reuses_the_page_fragment` covers all three paths; 222 `modules` tests, both kernel architectures, and serial-only smokes passed before merge. | B1770 |
| FIXED ff7fdebd3 | med | Virtio ring-pointer proofs across blk/gpu/rng/vsock/snd/net depended on a queue-size cap hidden in the queue programmer. | FIXED 566807b61 | med | Virtio drivers accepted queues too small for their published descriptor ids. | B1774 adds `require_queue_at_least`; VSOCK requires 8 RX descriptors, blk 3, GPU 4 control/2 cursor, and sound 2 control/3 PCM. 58 virtio tests, affected-driver checks, and the full pre-push gate passed. | B1774 |
| FIXED dcf0e7dbf | high | `systemd-resolved` allocated no DNS scope during boot despite a valid link, DHCP address, DNS server and route. The stale B1760/B1749/B1738/C275/D476 rows all described this one defect from different probes. | B1761 identified the Linux ABI omission: multicast delivery discarded `NETLINK_CB(skb).dst_group` and `recvmsg` emitted no `SOL_NETLINK` / `NETLINK_PKTINFO` cmsg. systemd-resolved enables that option and dispatches its route callbacks by packet-info group, so it classified every notification as group zero. Queueing and copyout now preserve the group. Serial-only x86 verification reports `Current Scopes: DNS LLMNR/IPv4` and `Current DNS Server: 10.0.2.3` without restarting resolved; x86_64 and aarch64 kernel checks pass. | B1761 |
| FIXED 068551621, dcf0e7dbf | high | Name resolution was reported as still failing through the `127.0.0.53` systemd-resolved stub after DHCP had supplied `10.0.2.3`. | The original observation predated the loopback-bind fix and netlink packet-info fix. B1768 serial-only probe on current main ran `getent hosts one.one.one.one` inside the guest and returned `2606:4700:4700::1001` and `2606:4700:4700::1111`; it therefore traversed the guest resolver path successfully. | B1768 |
| FIXED B1655 | med | `TCP_ZEROCOPY_RECEIVE` returned ENOPROTOOPT. Needed mm-side page mapping into the caller's address space. | B1655. `mmap(2)` on a TCP socket fd now builds a receive window (read-only, `EPERM` on write/exec, `ENODEV` for every other socket); the option remaps whole receive-queue pages into it. Operand layout, optlen versioning, errno ordering, copy-buffer fallback and straggler, `length`/`recv_skip_hint`/`inq`/`err` rules covered by 26 hosted tests in `net::sock_opts::sol_tcp::zerocopy::tests` plus 6 in `syscalls::tcp_zerocopy::window::tests`. | B1655 |

| Status | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |

| FIXED 402793b5a | med | Landlock slots 444/445 had no live coverage of fault recovery in their target-gated `copy_from_user` paths; slot 446's live privilege/flag/fd ladder was likewise unproven. | One portable probe now drives cross-page faults through create head, create extension tail, path rule and network rule copies, plus the query/size/tail and restrict-self orderings. Every record matches the Linux oracle on x86_64 and aarch64. Positive control replaced only the ABI-10 guest's faulting head pointer and turned the frame red (`EFAULT` vs `ENOMSG`), then restoration returned green. | B1849-landlock-uaccess-conformance |
| FIXED 402793b5a | low | Landlock's correct ABI pin named external implementation paths instead of limiting repository documentation to the observable compatibility baseline and invariants. | `docs/27§6` now pins Linux 7.2 / Landlock ABI 10 without external implementation paths. The existing code/doc drift test and the new exact in-guest `landlock_create_ruleset(...VERSION)` probe both require ABI 10. | B1849-landlock-uaccess-conformance |
| FIXED 833887264 | low | Landlock erratum 3 was not advertised because the ledger said refer checks omitted the mount-point hierarchy after reaching a directory disconnected from a bind root. | Re-audit found the implementation already carries the reference's two-stage mask walk: `collect_domain_accesses` consumes the disconnected ancestry, then `walk::from` resumes at the saved mount root and crosses its attachment hierarchy. A real refer decision test splits required rights across disjoint trees: both halves allow, either half alone returns `EACCES`; deleting the second walk turns the allow into `EXDEV`. Erratum bit 3 is now reported and locked to `docs/27§6`; 108 Landlock tests and the spec-lint ratchet pass. | B1848-landlock-disconnected-hierarchy |
| FIXED a313bde28 | high | `LANDLOCK_RESTRICT_SELF_TSYNC` directly overwrote sibling domains during one best-effort registry walk, so a concurrent clone escaped the policy and racing TSYNC callers could leave a mixed thread group. | Siblings now run pseudo-signal task work on their own return-to-user path, park at preparation and commit barriers, and are rediscovered until a full scan is empty. TSYNC and exec share one process writer exclusion; contenders restart before mutation. The clone-round and ABI-status positive controls fail with the defect restored; 1,193 scheduler tests, 107 Landlock tests, both kernel builds, and both frame/stack gates pass. | B1847-landlock-tsync-atomic |
| FIXED 4ecabf32d | med | Landlock's target ABI was not pinned independently of the kernel's `uname(2)` release, and the live ledger still claimed ABI 8 after the implementation had advanced to ABI 10. | `docs/27§6` pins Linux 7.2 / Landlock ABI 10, records every cumulative rung and open mismatch, and names `uapi::TARGET_ABI_VERSION` as the code owner. A regression test reads the documented target and fails on drift; its red/green positive control passed. | B1846-landlock-abi-10-contract |

## Memory / MM

| Status | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| FIXED 2b44a8a29 | high | **No architecture-neutral write-combining policy existed; x86 programmed no PAT and arm64 exposed no Normal-NC leaf policy, so a physical framebuffer could only be mapped strongly uncached.** | x86 now programs one Linux-compatible PAT policy on the BSP and every AP, including the legacy Intel errata layout and correct 4 KiB/large-leaf encodings. arm64 MAIR owns Normal-NC AttrIdx2. Raw-PFN VMAs carry one driver-selected write-back, write-combining, or device policy through the production fault path; PMM-backed virtio scanout remains write-back while physical framebuffer drivers can select WC without a second registry. Focused suites pass 155 x86 HAL, 149 arm64 HAL, 337 VMM, and 29 fbdev tests; paired release smoke passed first attempt, and Firefox run `2088783` rendered valid/invalid pages in 4.950/1.461 s at 0.153-0.155 us kernel time per syscall. Measurements are retained in `scratch/write-combining-performance-20260806.md`. | B1874-x86-write-combining |
| FIXED F822 | med | A private file mapping's COW pages had no anonymous reverse-map owner, so the default core filter could reduce modified data to an identifying header page. | Private COW installation now creates and attaches the VMA's canonical anonymous rmap owner, records the new page there, and marks VMA-owned private-page history. VMM and coredump selection regressions cover the classification. | F822-coredump-mapping-classification |
| FIXED F822 | med | io_uring and aio ring mappings backed by a kernel frame classified as private memory with no backing and were omitted from core dumps. | The canonical KernelFrame backing is classified as its shared mapped object class; the coredump adapter regression pins the mapped-shared filter decision. | F822-coredump-mapping-classification |
| FIXED F822 | low | An anonymous mapping became indistinguishable from untouched memory after all resident pages were evicted, so the default filter omitted it. | VMA-owned anonymous-page history, rather than RSS, now drives the private filter class and survives reclaim. | F822-coredump-mapping-classification |
| FIXED F823 | med | A `PT_LOAD` boundary page containing both file bytes and `.bss` was split into kernel-owned bytes, so it no longer classified as file-backed. | The VMA retains the whole file-backed boundary page; its canonical per-mapping backing delegates object identity and returns zeroes past the segment's file extent. Focused loader regressions cover the mapping boundary and byte contents. | F823-elf-load-bss-boundary-mapping |
| FIXED F824 | med | A position-independent image with no interpreter kept kernel-owned backing for every segment because the loader applied its relative relocations before entry. | The loader now maps untouched executable file bytes and transfers control; the no-interpreter position-independent entry path owns its own relocation. A biased file-backed PIE regression pins the mapping class. | F824-elf-pie-self-relocation |
| FIXED F825 | low | Later `NT_PRSTATUS` records repeated the process CPU-time snapshot, losing each worker thread's user and system CPU time. | Each thread snapshot now owns its `utime`/`stime`; the live capture reads the group sample for a leader, own counters for a non-leader, and shared child accounting for `cutime`/`cstime`. The two-thread note regression proves the records differ where required. | F825-coredump-thread-times |
| FIXED F826 | med | x86-64 core dumps emitted no `NT_X86_XSTATE` note even though the note assembler supported one. | Live capture synchronizes the canonical task FPU buffer once, emits its leading legacy region as `NT_PRFPREG`, and emits the complete XSAVE image unchanged as `NT_X86_XSTATE` when extended state is active. The note regression pins the complete descriptor and its header bytes. Hosted coredump tests, both feature targets, and paired smoke passed. | F826-coredump-x86-xstate |
| FIXED F828 | med | The socket (`@`) destination delivered nothing. A pattern beginning with `@` silently produced no dump and no file. | Direct socket patterns now validate and expand one absolute pathname, resolve its socket inode from the initial mount namespace, connect through the initial AF_UNIX owner, send the complete image without SIGPIPE, then shut down the write side. Hosted pathname validation and both kernel target gates pass. | F828-coredump-socket-delivery |
| FIXED F829 | med | The `@@` coredump-socket request/ack protocol was absent, so a collector could not select kernel generation, userspace generation, rejection, or an exit wait. | The socket ABI owner now emits the 16-byte feature request, validates size, spare, supported mask, and exactly one primary owner, then returns the required 32-bit marker. Negotiation runs before image construction, so userspace and reject choices build no kernel image; kernel/userspace choices shut down write and honor wait. Direct `@` wait follows `core_pipe_limit`. Ten hosted protocol regressions, a red/green positive control, both kernel builds, feature/frame/task-stack/IRQ gates, and per-crate host/test builds pass. | F829-coredump-socket-request-ack |

## Process

Retired to CLAUDE.md as standing hard rules (`Conflict resolution is where
coverage dies`, `Verification must be able to fail`) — a lesson that lives only
in a ledger row is one nobody reads before the next merge.

| Status | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| FIXED 611f4df7b | high | Three concurrent futex tests drove one process-global fake clock, allowing one test to move another's deadline backwards and park it forever after a spurious wake. | A guard serializes the clock-driving tests and resets time on acquisition while remaining held across spawned waiters. Its exclusion/reset regression is deterministic; 25 repeated focused runs and the 13,156-test workspace passed. | B1742-futex-test-clock-isolation |
| FIXED 39e78b9de | low | Two `/proc/<pid>` inode-number tags collided: the projected-id map file and the personality file shared tag `0x2e`, so the two files of one process shared an inode number. | `projid_map` now uses an unused tag while `personality` retains `0x2e`; the shared tag constants and a hosted regression prove distinct inode identities across representative PIDs. `cargo test -p procfs --lib` passed (163 tests), along with the full pre-push gates. | D494-procfs-pid-inode-tag-collision-ledger |
| FIXED D439 | med | Two lanes splitting one file for the size cap along different axes produces a conflict where the STALE side looks plausible. B1641 and B1649 both split `procfs/ctl.rs`; B1649's copy of the `net` subtree predated B1641 making `rmem_default`/`wmem_default` live and adding `tcp_rmem`/`tcp_wmem`, so resolving line-by-line would have silently reverted two leaves to dead `Const` and deleted two more, with nothing red. Rule: take the other side wholesale and re-apply your own delta; verify by MULTISET count, not name set. | B1649 merge of `27cdb991f`; 100 declarations both sides, none dropped, none duplicated. | D439 |
| FIXED D439 | med | Verification that returns green without exercising what it claims to. Two confirmed instances: `procfs/ctl.rs` is target-gated so `cargo check` compiled none of it (break appeared only in the kernel build); a set-based duplicate-test check that structurally could not detect duplicates (a set dedupes the very thing being looked for). Prefer a positive control — reinstate the defect and confirm the check goes red. | B1641. | D439 |

| Status | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| FIXED F777 | high | Four `RLIMIT_*` defaults were `RLIM_INFINITY` where Linux's `INIT_RLIMITS` (after `fork_init`'s two `max_threads/2` overwrites) sets them otherwise: `NICE` and `RTPRIO` are `{0,0}`, `MSGQUEUE` is `{819200,819200}`, `NPROC` is `{max_threads/2, ...}`. NICE/RTPRIO are the load-bearing pair — the `setpriority(2)` and `sched_setscheduler(2)` ladders read exactly those slots and are written correctly, so an infinite default made both structurally unable to refuse anything and any unprivileged process could take nice -20 or SCHED_FIFO 99 with no `CAP_SYS_NICE`. A procfs test had pinned the wrong MSGQUEUE value as if it were correct. | F777. 16-slot `INIT_RLIMITS` conformance table in `sched::tests::rlimit_prio`, plus a `nice_to_rlimit` allowance test proving the default admits no priority increase; procfs render test repointed. | F777 |
| FIXED F777 | high | `setrlimit(2)` and `prlimit64(2)` gated the hard-limit RAISE on `has_cap(CAP_SYS_RESOURCE)` — a plain effective-set test — where Linux asks `capable()` = `ns_capable(&init_user_ns, ...)`. Root inside an unprivileged user namespace could therefore hand itself any hard limit after one `unshare(CLONE_NEWUSER)`. `perm_common::capable` already encoded the correct test and neither slot called it: machinery without callers. | F777. Both slots now route through the ungated `rlimit_policy::cap_sys_resource`; two new tests drive that exact helper over a real `sched::Task` moved into and out of a non-initial user namespace. | F777 |
| FIXED b8d233ca8 | med | `TCP_DEFER_ACCEPT` completed the handshake and withheld the child from `accept`, so the peer observed ESTABLISHED while its bare acknowledgement should have left the request half-open. | A listener now keeps a SYN-RECV request socket outside the accept queue; a bare acknowledgement is deferred, while data or the final solicited acknowledgement promotes it. Request-timer and end-to-end defer-accept regressions cover the state and peer-visible handover. | B1656-tcp-defer-accept-minisock |
| FIXED B1656 | high | `net` did not compile for the plain `cargo check -p net` target: `sock_v6_name` is ungated but imports the socket type, which only the kernel, test and hosted configurations have. Every lane's `cargo test -p net` failed to build the lib target on top of it. | Reproduced on a clean `origin/main` worktree. Fixed by giving the module the same gate as the type it reads — the gate still includes `test`, so nothing that exercised it stops being exercised. | B1656 |
| FIXED F779 | high | Child resource accounting (`getrusage(RUSAGE_CHILDREN)`, `times(2)`'s `tms_cutime`/`tms_cstime`) folded a child into its parent at EXIT rather than at REAP. `RUSAGE_CHILDREN` covers children that terminated AND were waited for, so three things were wrong: an unreaped zombie was already counted; a `waitid(WNOWAIT)` peek left the child waitable but accounted (systemd's peek-then-reap SIGCHLD handler is the real caller); and an auto-reaped child (`SIGCHLD`=`SIG_IGN` / `SA_NOCLDWAIT`) was accounted at all, which upstream never does. | F779 (#4338). Fold moved to the one reap arm that consumes the zombie, gated on thread-group leadership, crediting the reaper. 7 tests in `sched::tests::child_rusage` over the real zombie list; both pre-existing exit-time tests went red against the fix before being rewritten. | F779 |
| FIXED F779 | med | The `CLK_TCK` divisor was open-coded in four places that must agree by construction — `AT_CLKTCK` (what `sysconf(_SC_CLK_TCK)` returns), the `times(2)` / `/proc/<pid>/stat` conversion, and two in `/proc/uptime`'s centisecond math. A split source of truth whose only symptom is userspace's `ticks / sysconf(_SC_CLK_TCK)` being quietly wrong. | F779 (#4338). `USER_HZ` / `NS_PER_USER_TICK` / `ns_to_clock_t` moved to the leaf ABI crate `syscall::rusage`; all four consumers read them; 3 new tests. | F779 |

Replaced by the ungated `syscall::rusage::getrusage_source` returning a 3-variant enum; dispatch is now exhaustive and hosted-tested. | F779 |

Two locks over one resource exclude nothing, so a `kernel/tests.rs` test and a crate-root `tests.rs` test ran concurrently and clobbered each other's flush probe, registration and pending bit. `kernel/tests.rs`'s own comment claimed it serialized "against the rest of the crate" — it could not. Four locks over one resource exclude nothing. Tests also hand-picked slot numbers, so two different devices both claimed `5`, and ids `9`/`10`/`11` are outside the table entirely. | FIXED B1684 | med | No fast-open option existed in the header codec at all: neither the assigned kind 34 nor the RFC 6994 experimental form (kind 254, experiment id `0xF989`). A peer offering either was invisible, and this side could not have answered one. | `tcp_hdr::opt` carried only MSS / WScale / SACK-permitted / SACK / Timestamp. Now `parse_fastopen_option` reads both forms and `SynOptions::fastopen` writes both, byte-pinned for every cookie length under both kinds. | B1684 |
| FIXED B1684 | med | `parse_wscale_option` clamped a peer's advertised window scale with a bare literal `14`, duplicating `sol_tcp::TCP_MAX_WSCALE`. Two copies of one RFC limit that could drift, and a magic number in a position `07§5` names explicitly. | Now one owner, `tcp_hdr::WSCALE_MAX`, which `TCP_MAX_WSCALE` aliases; `a_peer_scale_above_the_ceiling_is_clamped_on_the_wire` asserts both the clamp and that the two constants are the same value, and fails when the clamp is removed. | B1684 |
| FIXED B1685 | med | **`name_to_handle_at` on cgroupfs returned EOVERFLOW**, so the service manager could not read any cgroup id (`Failed to get cgroup ID of cgroup /sys/fs/cgroup/system.slice/<unit>, ignoring: Value too large for defined data type` on every unit start). Root cause: the FID codec was VFS-global — one 12-byte `(ino, generation)` payload for every filesystem — while the cgroup-id reader sizes its buffer to `sizeof(uint64_t)` = 8 and does not run the grow-and-retry protocol, so every call overflowed. Fixed by adding the per-superblock encode/decode half of `s_export_op` (the decode half already existed) and giving cgroup2 the kernfs handle: 8 bytes, `FILEID_KERNFS`, payload = the node id, which is the directory's inode number. | B1663 boot log, ~40 occurrences from `system-getty.slice` onward. Now: 9 hosted tests in `cgroup::export::tests` + 4 in `vfs::export::kernfs_fid::tests`. | B1685 |
| FIXED F784 | high | The compliance matrix tracked 319 of 385 native syscalls while its own header claimed 385, so every `IMPL`/`PARTIAL` count taken from it was computed over 83% of the surface, and a lane sent to close a row that did not exist could read the absence as "already done" (that happened on `F782`). All 66 added at `NEEDS-AUDIT` with mechanically-derived routes; a re-runnable completeness check is now in the header. | F784. Post-pass: 385 rows, untracked set `NONE`, columns intact, diff is insertions only. | F784 |
| FIXED B1687 | high | **gnome-control-center dies with SIGSEGV** (`exit=139`, `timeout: the monitored command dumped core`) ~25 s after exec, with EMPTY stderr — no GLib warning, no assertion, no message of its own. Kernel klog shows no `[FAULT]` line because that dump is `debug-irq`-gated, and no core file is written (see the `core_pattern` row). | — |
| FIXED B1687 | high | **nautilus dies with SIGSEGV** (`exit=139`) at window realisation. Bisected to the GTK4 renderer: `GSK_RENDERER=cairo` -> survives 60 s (`exit=124`), `GSK_RENDERER=ngl` -> survives (`124`), `GSK_RENDERER=vulkan` -> SIGSEGV (`139`). So the fault is on the Vulkan path only; the app itself, wayland, dbus, dconf, gvfs and the GL/software renderers are all fine. | `G_MESSAGES_DEBUG=all` log reaches `nautilus-window-DEBUG: Setting new slot ... as active` then dies. Under `GSK_RENDERER=vulkan` + `VK_LOADER_DEBUG=all` the last line is the loader printing the `vkCreateInstance` layer callstack down to `<Drivers>` — i.e. it dies inside driver instance creation / physical-device enumeration, after all 11 mesa ICD manifests are found. | — |
| FIXED B1687 | high | The Vulkan SIGSEGV needs the FULL mesa ICD set: restricting the loader to one ICD (`VK_DRIVER_FILES=lvp_icd...`) survives, to `virtio_icd` survives, and to `lvp:virtio` together survives (`exit=124` each) — but with all 11 ICDs enumerated it faults (`139`). `NODEVICE_SELECT=1` does NOT help (`139`), so `VK_LAYER_MESA_device_select` is ruled out. The crasher is therefore one of the nine hardware ICDs (asahi, broadcom, freedreno, intel_hasvk, intel, nouveau, panfrost, powervr_mesa, radeon) probing the DRM device, which on a machine with no matching hardware must enumerate zero devices and return cleanly. Suspected kernel side: what `/dev/dri/renderD128` + its sysfs identity (`/sys/dev/char/226:128/device/{subsystem,vendor,device,...}`) report to `drmGetDevices2()`, letting a wrong-vendor ICD claim the node. | B1663 in-guest bisect, all on one boot. Per-ICD single-driver runs partially complete at time of filing. | — |
| FIXED B1687 | high | The GTK4 Vulkan SIGSEGV is caused by ONE mesa ICD, `powervr_mesa` — not by the ICD count and not by the DRM device. ANY pair containing it faults (`lvp+powervr` 139, `panfrost+powervr` 139); pairs without it are clean (`lvp+virtio` 124); the driver ALONE is clean (124). Adding it to a clean set of seven flips that set to 139, adding `panfrost` instead does not. An alternate set of NINE without it survives, so it is not a count threshold. `chmod 000 /dev/dri/renderD128` does NOT prevent the fault, so the render node and every DRM ioctl are ruled out. `NODEVICE_SELECT=1` does not help, so `VK_LAYER_MESA_device_select` is ruled out. | B1663 in-guest bisect: 11 single-ICD runs, five set sizes, an alternate-nine control, two eight-ICD runs, a trio, two pairs, one render-node-denied run. | — |
| FIXED B1687 | med | The "large `PT_TLS`" lead for the `powervr_mesa` SIGSEGV does NOT survive its own follow-up check and must not be treated as the cause. `libpowervr_rogue.so` (4352 B of TLS, faults) and `libvulkan_radeon.so` (48 B, does NOT fault) use the IDENTICAL relocation type — `R_X86_64_TLSDESC`, two vs three of them. Same `dlopen` code path, same lazy `__tls_get_addr` resolution, no initial-exec relocation anywhere, so no static-TLS-surplus failure is involved in either. The libraries differ only in TLS SIZE, and 4352 B is not a large allocation. Anyone picking this up should treat the TLS angle as unconfirmed and start from the faulting `ip` instead. | B1663/B1667, `readelf -rW` on both libraries extracted from the image. Recorded because a negative result here saves the next lane the same detour. | — |
| FIXED B1681 | HIGH | Every pipe-destination dump was silently truncated at the first full ring. The ring was a fixed 4 KiB inline array and the blocking write returned `Erestartsys` as soon as any signal was deliverable — permanently true for the thread the core dumper runs on, since it is inside the delivery of the signal that killed it. A multi-megabyte dump therefore aborted at 4 KiB and `write_all` reported failure. This is why `coredumctl list` stayed empty with a valid `core_pattern` and a listening socket. | `pipe::ring::tests::a_fresh_ring_holds_the_default_pipe_size_not_one_page`; ring default now 64 KiB, grown on demand to a 1 MiB ceiling; `WriteAbort::OnFatalKill` gates the dumper's wait on `sched::live::fatal_kill_pending_self` | B1681 |
| FIXED B1681 | MED | `/proc/sys/kernel/core_pipe_limit` was a procfs-local `Int` cell with no reader anywhere in the tree — an operator could set a concurrency cap and every dump would ignore it. | `coredump::limits::tests::*` (6); leaf rebound to `IntHook(hooks::core_pipe_limit, …)`; `dump_to_program` claims a slot before starting a helper | B1681 |
| FIXED B1681 | HIGH | `write_to_file` wrote no file. It created an anonymous tmpfs inode and registered a `Box::leak`ed path string in devfs — never touching the real namespace, never honouring the mode, and leaking the path on every dump. | `coredump::file::tests::*` (10) + `coredump/file_target.rs`: namespace walk, exclusive create at mode 0600, admission ladder, chunked write | B1681 |
| FIXED B1681 | MED | `F_SETPIPE_SZ` returned `EPERM` for any size above 4096, so no program could enlarge a pipe. Linux reports 65536 on a fresh pipe and allows up to `pipe-max-size`. | `pipe::ring::tests::resizing_reports_at_least_what_was_asked_for_and_refuses_the_ceiling` | B1681 |
| FIXED B1681 | MED | POSIX `PIPE_BUF` atomicity was keyed to the ring capacity, so raising the capacity would have silently widened the atomicity guarantee to the whole ring. Now a separate constant. | `pipe::ring::tests::a_write_up_to_pipe_buf_is_all_or_nothing` | B1681 |
| FIXED B1681 | LOW | A `PipeBuf` was 12 KiB of inline arrays constructed on the stack inside `Arc::new(PipeData::new(..))` — a large transient stack frame on 16 KiB kernel stacks, on the same allocation path guard pages were added for. Backing store is now heap and lazily grown. | `pipe::ring::tests::the_backing_store_is_allocated_on_demand_not_up_front` | B1681 |
| FIXED B1687 | CRITICAL | A PCI function published only `vendor`, `device`, `class`, `resource`, `modalias`, `driver_override` and `uevent`. libdrm's device enumeration counts devices on a pass that only needs the directory, then fetches the full identity on a second pass that needs `revision`, `subsystem_vendor`, `subsystem_device` (falling back to the raw `config` blob). The fetch pass dropped every device, so `drmGetDevices2(0, NULL, 0)` returned 1 and `drmGetDevices2(0, arr, 1)` returned 0 with a NULL slot. A Vulkan ICD that sizes its loop from the first count then dereferenced an unwritten slot, killing GTK4's default renderer — gnome-control-center and nautilus both SIGSEGV'd on start. | `bus::tests::pci_dev_attrs::*` (4); `bus::pci_attrs::*` (26); in-guest reproducer `drmGetDevices2` count/fetch now agree | B1687 |
| FIXED B1687 | HIGH | `config` did not exist at all: no raw config-space blob, so lspci/setpci and every libdrm/libpciaccess fallback path had nothing to read. Now a byte-granular attribute with the reference window rules — 64 bytes for an unprivileged reader (128 for a CardBus bridge), the whole space for `CAP_SYS_ADMIN` in the initial user namespace — and access past the window is a short read, never an error. | `bus::pci_attrs::config::tests::*` (5); `pci::config_space::tests::*` (10) | B1687 |
| FIXED B1687 | MED | The `resource` table emitted one row per *present* BAR, so a function whose BAR0 is unset shifted every later row up and a parser indexing rows by BAR number read the wrong window. | FIXED B1687 | MED | `irq`, `local_cpus`, `local_cpulist`, `numa_node`, `enable`, `power_state`, `dma_mask_bits`, `consistent_dma_mask_bits`, `msi_bus`, `broken_parity_status`, `ari_enabled`, `boot_vga`, `remove`, `rescan` and the bridge bus-number pair were all absent. | FIXED C256 | HIGH | **The N22 conformance channel was measuring our libc, not our kernel.** `xtask glibc-test` called `sysroot::build_sysroot`, which built `crates/user/{glibc,ldso,folded-stub,crt1}` into `target/sysroot/<triple>/lib`, then linked each guest binary `-nostdlib -Wl,--dynamic-linker=/opt/oxide-conformance/lib/ld-linux-* -l:libc.so.6` and shipped that libc into the image. So a guest-vs-oracle mismatch could be a bug in our from-scratch glibc rather than in the kernel, and there was no way to tell which from the result. The guest binary is now an ordinary dynamically-linked PIE with the platform loader path, resolved by the image's own Fedora `ld-linux` + `libc.so.6` — the same pair every other program in the guest uses. | `file t_mmsg.x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu.guest` → `interpreter /lib64/ld-linux-x86-64.so.2`; aarch64 → `interpreter /lib/ld-linux-aarch64.so.1`; 218/218 oracles recorded + guest binaries linked on both arches | C256 |

All four are gone now that `conformance` no longer builds a sysroot. | 661 LOC deleted; `cargo check -p xtask` clean with `crates/user/ldso` dropped from `tools/xtask/Cargo.toml` | C256 |

| FIXED B1687 | high | What is unique about `powervr_mesa`: it is the only ICD whose `DT_NEEDED` list carries `libpowervr_rogue.so`, and that library (present, 9.4 MB) is the only one in the set with a LARGE `PT_TLS` segment — `p_memsz 0x1100` (4352 B) of zero-init TLS. `libvulkan_radeon.so` also has `PT_TLS` but only 0x30 (48 B) and does not fault; `lvp`, `virtio`, `intel`, `panfrost` and `powervr_mesa` itself have none. 4352 B is far past glibc's static-TLS surplus, so this is the one `dlopen` in the whole enumeration that forces the heavy TLS path (static-surplus placement or DTV growth) in an already-multithreaded process. | FIXED B1687 | med | The static-TLS-surplus explanation for the `powervr_mesa` SIGSEGV is refuted outright, by two independent arguments. (1) That failure shape REQUIRES initial-exec TLS relocations — those are the ones resolved at load time out of the static block, and exhausting it is what makes `dlopen` fail. There are **zero** `R_X86_64_TPOFF64` relocations in ANY of the twelve libraries in the set. Every TLS reference is `R_X86_64_TLSDESC`, i.e. lazy general-dynamic, allocated on demand and never from the surplus, so the surplus is not consumed by these `dlopen`s at all. (2) A controlled comparison: `libvulkan_asahi.so` has the IDENTICAL `PT_TLS` `p_memsz 0x1100` (4352 B) and the same 2 TLSDESC relocations as `libpowervr_rogue.so`, and does NOT fault — it is clean alone and present in every clean seven-ICD set. `libvulkan_freedreno.so` carries a LARGER `PT_TLS` (`0x1160`, 3 TLSDESC) and also does not fault. TLS size is not the discriminator. Separately, the shape does not even match the symptom: static-TLS exhaustion makes `dlopen` return an error the Vulkan loader handles by skipping the ICD, it does not SIGSEGV. | B1663/B1667: `readelf -lW` + `readelf -rW` across all 11 ICDs and `libpowervr_rogue.so`; behavioural control from the single-ICD run matrix. | — |

Note the split: `libvulkan_powervr_mesa.so` itself has NO `PT_TLS`; the TLS lives in that dependency. So the distinguishing property is "one extra large shared object mapped during ICD enumeration", not anything about TLS. Next step is the faulting `ip`, not more inference. | B1663/B1667, `readelf -dW` across the set. | — |

| FIXED B1691 | high | **Unbounded write into the caller's `args` array in `linux_string/match_parser.rs`.** `pattern_match` stored one `substring_t` per `%` conversion in the pattern with no cap. | FIXED B1692 | HIGH | `program_queue` used the DEVICE-supplied `queue_size` unchecked while allocating exactly ONE frame per ring area. | FIXED B1692 | HIGH | DRM ioctls dereferenced USER pointers raw (`read_volatile(arg as *const T)`) behind nothing but a range check. A range check proves the address is in the user half, not that it is mapped readable; such a dereference from kernel mode has no exception-table entry, so the fault handler finds no VMA, `handled=false`, and the CPU OOPSes and halts — an unprivileged local DoS via any bogus ioctl pointer. It also imposed the struct's alignment on a pointer userspace chose. Converted `dumb/ioctl.rs`, `node/atomic_ioctl.rs` and `kms_ext.rs` cursor paths to a new `drm::uarg` that goes through `uaccess::copy_{from,to}_user` (exception-table-backed) and `read_unaligned`. | `drm::uarg::tests::a_null_user_pointer_is_reported_not_dereferenced`, `…::every_drm_ioctl_arg_struct_fits_the_transfer_buffer`; `crates/kernel/uaccess/src/copy.rs` vs `hal-x86_64/src/fault.rs:229` (fixup only for exception-table RIPs) | B1692 |
| FIXED B1692 | HIGH | `MODE_ATOMIC` summed the per-object property counts in `atomic_property_count`, range-checked `props_ptr`/`prop_values_ptr` against that total, then RE-READ each per-object count from the same user array in the copy loop. Userspace rewriting `count_props[]` between the two passes walked the flattened index past the checked extent, reading `props_ptr + pos*4` outside it — a user-controlled out-of-range read whose values reach `atomic::commit`. The running total is now re-checked per object. | `node::atomic_ioctl::tests::a_count_grown_since_validation_is_refused` + `an_overflowing_running_total_is_refused`; positive control: deleting the `end > count` guard makes the first FAIL | B1692 |

| FIXED F789 | high | **The syscall compliance matrix tracks only 319 of 385 real x86_64 syscalls — 66 are absent entirely**, so every coverage statistic derived from it (`IMPL`/`PARTIAL` counts, "what is left") is computed over 83% of the surface and silently understates it. So this is a TRACKING gap, not a code gap, which is the dangerous kind: a lane assigned "close the PARTIAL rows for clone/execve" finds no such rows and may conclude the work is done. That happened on this lane — 4 of 10 assigned rows did not exist. Untracked: 0:read, 2:open, 9:mmap, 15:rt_sigreturn, 22:pipe, 26:msync, 28:madvise, 29:shmget, 35:nanosleep, 45:recvfrom, 48:shutdown, 56:clone, 59:execve, 64:semget, 65:semop, 67:shmdt, 68:msgget, 69:msgsnd, 70:msgrcv, 72:fcntl, 73:flock, 82:rename, 85:creat, 94:lchown, 101:ptrace, 117:setresuid, 126:capset, 132:utime, 141:setpriority, 149:mlock, 153:vhangup, 157:prctl, 161:chroot, 167:swapon, 188:setxattr, 189:lsetxattr, 190:fsetxattr, 203:sched_setaffinity, 216:remap_file_pages, 218:set_tid_address, 219:restart_syscall, 230:clock_nanosleep, 233:epoll_ctl, 240:mq_open, 241:mq_unlink, 242:mq_timedsend, 243:mq_timedreceive, 244:mq_notify, 245:mq_getsetattr, 248:add_key, 249:request_key, 254:inotify_add_watch, 264:renameat, 272:unshare, 279:move_pages, 288:accept4, 289:signalfd4, 290:eventfd2, 293:pipe2, 294:inotify_init1, 309:getcpu, 316:renameat2, 318:getrandom, 319:memfd_create, 322:execveat, 452:fchmodat2. (2) `quota_listener()` returned `(socket, guard)`, and bindings drop in reverse declaration order, so `let (listener, _serial)` released the claim while the socket was still registered in the process-global `GENL_LISTENERS` list — the exclusion has to outlive the resource it protects. Each task now takes its tid as its account uid — which is what Linux gives a test its own account: a distinct user, not a lock. | `push()` refused the 1st of 3 records under a limit of 3. 1/10 workspace runs before, 0/30 after. | — |

| FIXED B1704 | high | **The `request_key(2)` construction never completed on a booted system** because the assumed instantiation token did not survive the helper's own credential transitions. `/sbin/request-key` assumes authority over the key, EXECS the handler its configuration names, and that handler FORKS `keyctl instantiate` to answer. The exec hook DROPPED the token and the fork hook never COPIED it, so the program that finally answers held no authority and `instantiate_core` returned EPERM — the helper exited non-zero and the key was left under construction, then negated. Neither hook is what the reference does: `prepare_exec_creds` touches only the thread and process keyrings, and `prepare_creds` takes a reference to the same token for the child. | `keyring/lifecycle.rs` `fork`/`exec`; four positive controls, each reinstating one defect, go RED. | this lane |
| FIXED B1704 | high | Second half of the same failure, and the one the exec/fork fix exposed: with the token finally reaching `keyctl instantiate`, the answer had nowhere to go — the destination the stock handler names is `%S`, the requester's session keyring, which grants its owner View and Read through the user byte and Write only through the possessor byte. Possession was computed from the CALLER's keyrings only, so the helper was denied and the construction ended EACCES -> negate -> ENOKEY. `lookup_user_key` decides possession by searching the process keyrings, which follows the token into the requesting task's keyrings. In-guest before: `[KEYRING] instantiate tid=4282 rc=-13`; after: `rc=0`. | `keyring/perm.rs` `is_possessed`; `chain::a_helper_caches_the_answer_in_the_requester_s_session_keyring`, control RED. | this lane |
| FIXED B1704 | med | A helper had no reach into the REQUESTER's keyrings: only `search_cred_keyrings_rcu` existed, never `search_process_keyrings_rcu`, so a handler needing one of the requesting task's keys found nothing. Added `ops::search::search_process` — own keyrings, then the requester's under the requester's credentials while a live token is held, with the outcomes merged by the same `success > -ENOKEY > -EAGAIN > other` priority. A TOKEN is deliberately not reachable that way. | `keyring/ops/search.rs`; `chain::the_requester_reach_is_token_scoped_and_never_yields_a_token`, controls with the guard forced open and the fallback forced closed both go RED. | this lane |

| FIXED B1706 | high | `ptrace` read the x86_64 tracee's saved entry frame as **16 quadwords at `kernel_stack - 0x80`** while the frame the entry asm pushes is **22 quadwords at `kernel_stack - 0xb0`**. Consequence was silent wrong data, not a crash: `PTRACE_GETREGS` / `PTRACE_GETREGSET(NT_PRSTATUS)` / `PTRACE_PEEKUSER` reported the frame's `r11` as `orig_rax` and its `rcx` as `rip`, and `PTRACE_SETREGS` / `POKEUSER` wrote a debugged process's registers into the wrong slots. Invisible at a syscall stop (the `syscall` instruction genuinely parks the user RIP in `rcx`), visible at every fault/signal stop — i.e. exactly the attach a debugger performs. Fixed by deriving both the base and the field access from `PtRegs`, the struct the entry path pushes. | `t_ptrace_getregs` (new, `userspace/glibc_conformance/`): `pc_in_crash_fn` and `setregs_redirected_pc` at a SIGSEGV stop. Hosted: `s101_ptrace_regs::tests::x86_tests::*` (14 tests, was 9). Positive control: `u[U_RIP] = f.rcx` turns `x86_frame_maps_onto_the_right_user_regs_fields` and `x86_round_trip_preserves_every_general_register` RED. | B1706 |
| FIXED B1706 | med | Split source of truth about register layout: the live ptrace path carried its own frame offset table AND its own `struct user_regs_struct` index table, both duplicated by the core-dump `NT_PRSTATUS` path. Two paths could (and did) tell different stories about the same task. Frame layout now has one owner per arch (`PtRegs` / `SvcFrame`, reached by field name — no offsets restated anywhere); the ABI index tables have one owner (`hal::uregs::{x86_64::user_regs, aarch64::user_pt_regs}`) that `syscalls/101_ptrace` and `fs/coredump/gregset` both consult. | `crates/arch/hal/src/uregs.rs`; both consumers now `pub use` the same constants. | B1706 |
| FIXED B1706 | med | `PTRACE_GETREGS` reported a stale `rax` and a bogus `orig_rax` at any NON-syscall stop. `orig_rax` came from a frame slot that has no syscall meaning on a trap frame, and `rax` always came from the task's `ptrace_stop_rax` cell, which is only written at syscall stops — so a tracer stopped on SIGSEGV read whatever the last syscall stop left there. Now follows the frame's entry tag: on a `syscall` frame `rax` is the recorded stop value and `orig_rax` the syscall number; on a trap frame `rax` is the architectural register and `orig_rax` is the no-syscall marker, which is what the core-dump block already reported for the same frame. | `x86_trap_frame_reports_no_syscall_and_the_architectural_rax`, `x86_setregs_on_a_trap_frame_installs_the_architectural_rax`. | B1706 |
| FIXED B1707 | high | `PTRACE_GETSIGINFO` reported every fault stop as a kill record and filled `si_addr` with the TRACER's pid (filed by B1706). Two causes, both fixed: (1) `101_ptrace/sig.rs::getsiginfo` wrote only the `_kill` arm (`signo@0`, `code@8`, `pid@16`, `uid@20`, `value@24`) and never consulted the record's `_sigfault`/`_sigsys`/`_sigpoll` arm, so `si_addr` — bytes 16..24 in the fault arm — was never written; (2) `101_ptrace/stop.rs::notify_with` substituted the tracer's pid into `info.pid` whenever it was zero, unconditionally, so on a SIGSEGV record that word landed in `si_addr`. A debugger could not tell a wild pointer from `kill(2)`. `getsiginfo` now renders through the one `siginfo_t` writer; `notify_with` publishes the record exactly as its producer built it, and the SYNTHESISED event records name the TRACEE (`task_pid_vnr(current)`, `current_uid()`) as the reference kernel does. | Hosted: `hal::siginfo::tests` (18, was 8) + `s101_ptrace_event::tests` (18, was 14). In-guest x86_64: `t_ptrace_siginfo` prints `signo_is_segv=1 code_is_fault=1 addr_matches_fault=1 addr_is_not_a_pid=1`, matching the host oracle. Positive control: deleting the `_sigfault` arm from `write_siginfo` (the literal `_kill`-only write) turns 6 tests RED, one reporting `si_addr` = `0x99_00001234` — a pid and uid packed into the address field, the exact defect shape; deleting the reader's fault arm turns 3 RED. Both restored GREEN. | B1707 |
| FIXED B1707 | high | `PTRACE_SETSIGINFO` and every other `siginfo_t` copy-IN (`rt_sigqueueinfo`, `rt_tgsigqueueinfo`, `pidfd_send_signal`) decoded only the `_kill` arm, so a tracer's GETSIGINFO→SETSIGINFO round trip on a fault stop DESTROYED `si_addr` and re-read it as a sender's pid. The reference kernel's `kernel_siginfo_t` is the union itself, so its copy-in preserves the arm for free; ours is decomposed, so the arm is now recovered from `(si_signo, si_code)` by the same classifier the write side uses. | `hal::read_siginfo`; `every_arm_survives_a_flat_round_trip`, `the_caller_supplied_signal_selects_the_arm_not_the_buffers_si_signo`. | B1707 |
| FIXED B1707 | med | Split source of truth about `siginfo_t`: FOUR writers (`hal::write_siginfo`, `signal_common::write_user_siginfo`, `101_ptrace/sig.rs`, `101_ptrace/info.rs`), TWO readers (`signal_common::read_user_siginfo`, `101_ptrace/sig.rs`) and TWO independent copies of the `(signo, si_code)` → union-arm decision (`fs/signalfd/siginfo.rs`, and the implicit one in every hand-rolled `_kill` store). The two hand-rolled ptrace writers and the ptrace reader are deleted; `fs/signalfd` now consumes the classifier instead of carrying its own. One owner: `hal::siginfo` — `layout` (classify), `write_siginfo` (producer-tagged arm), `read_siginfo` (flat decode). | `crates/arch/hal/src/siginfo.rs`; `signalfd` tests unchanged and still green against the moved classifier. | B1707 |

The socket's `IpOpts` is now an `Arc` shared onto `TcpEntry.ip_opts` the way `ip_mtu_discover` is, so the area has one home; `TcpTxPolicy::ipv4_options` hands it to the IPv4 transmit path, and a compiled source route retargets the route lookup, the strict-route screen, the path MTU and the wire destination. Closes the curated rows "Sticky `IP_OPTIONS` do not reach TCP segments", "TCP does not emit IP_OPTIONS", and the residual caveat on the STALE "IP_OPTIONS never emitted on transmit" row. | `net::tests_tcp_ip_options_tx` (4 tests). | FIXED B1708 | med | The option area was not charged against the MSS, so a connection carrying one would have emitted segments that no longer fit the path MTU. `net::tcp_ext_hdr` is the ungated owner of the rule (subtract the area, then floor at the minimum send MSS — that order, not the reverse); it is applied at active open, at a learned-PMTU reduction, and at `NetStack::tcp_sync_mss`, which `IP_OPTIONS` set-time now calls for an established connection so a late `setsockopt` cannot leave a stale MSS. | `net::tcp_ext_hdr::tests` (4), `tests_tcp_ip_options_tx::the_sticky_option_area_is_charged_against_the_connections_mss`. | FIXED B1709 | med | `SO_NO_CHECK` was admitted, stored and readable with zero consumers: the IPv4 UDP builder always computed a checksum, so the option could not change a single wire byte. The flag now reaches `UdpHdr::build_into_opts` through the socket-owned transmit path (v4 and the v4-mapped AF_INET6 path), and the segmentation planner is handed the real value instead of a hardcoded `false`, so a socket asking for both `UDP_SEGMENT` and a suppressed checksum is refused rather than emitting unchecksummed segments. Closes the `SO_NO_CHECK has zero consumers` half of the curated `IP_CHECKSUM inert` row. | `net::udp::tests::suppressed_checksum_leaves_the_field_zero_and_the_datagram_acceptable`, `net::udp::tests::a_computed_zero_checksum_is_transmitted_as_all_ones`, `net::tests_udp_no_check_tx` (3). ## Retired by D473 — rows whose status already said closed

Moved verbatim from the live ledger. Column shape is the ledger's old
5-column form: `Status | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner`.

| Status | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| FIXED B1720 | high | `fsmount(2)` returns an anon-inode fd over a deferred mount object, not an `O_PATH` fd over a real vfsmount in an anonymous mount namespace. The fd carries `mnt_id == 0`, is `FileType::Regular` so it is `ENOTDIR` as a dirfd, is invisible to `statmount`/`listmount`, and between `fsmount` and `move_mount` the mount exists nowhere and can be neither observed nor referenced. `FSMOUNT_NAMESPACE` is unimplementable until that changes. Also missing: the `SB_NOUSER → EINVAL` check after mount creation, and `FMODE_NEED_UNMOUNT`/dissolve-on-close semantics. | `fsmount_common/fd.rs` `install_fd` → `vfs/src/file/model.rs` `File::new` (`mnt_id = 0`); `pathresolve/at.rs` rejects a non-directory dirfd. `move_mount` mode (a) grafts the superblock through a private-inode side channel instead. Blocks `432`. **Fixed by B1720:** `fsmount` now creates a REAL mount in an anonymous mount namespace (`create_anon_mount`) and returns an `O_PATH` fd over it, so the fd carries a real `mnt_id`, resolves as a dirfd, and dissolves its mount on close if never moved (`File`'s teardown → `dissolve_anon`, Linux's `__fput` → `dissolve_on_fput`). `move_mount` grafts that same mount, so the id is stable across the move. NOTE on the row's own text: invisibility to `statmount`/`listmount` was NOT a defect — `grab_requested_mnt_ns` selects the caller's namespace, so an anonymous mount is correctly invisible there. 9 hosted tests; positive control turns 3 red. | B1720 |
| FIXED B1719 | high | **`hidepid=` / `subset=pid` are NOT a parameter-table problem — procfs cannot express them at all.** Of the 22 registered filesystem types only 5 (`tmpfs`, `ramfs`, `ext4`, `autofs`, `fuse`) even receive the mount data string; the other 17, `proc` included, take `_` and discard it. And `procfs::static_files::proc_root()` returns a **process-global cached `Arc`**, so every `mount -t proc` in every namespace shares ONE root inode, leaving nowhere for per-mount option state. Per-mount `hidepid=` needs per-superblock root inodes first. **Consequence for the security framing: listing `hidepid` in a parameter table would NOT make `ProtectProc=` work — only implementing it does. Conversely a table that OMITS `hidepid` makes the support probe answer "no", and systemd then correctly stops claiming a confinement it is not getting.** Omission is the cheap honest fix; implementation is the real one. | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/fsmount_common/registry.rs:118` (proc ctor discards data); `crates/kernel/procfs/src/static_files.rs:73-76` (cached singleton root). **Fixed by B1719:** the singleton is gone — `ProcfsFs::new(info)` builds a root inode per mount carrying that mount's `Arc<ProcFsInfo>`, published in `sb.s_fs_info` as the reference does. `hidepid=`/`subset=`/`gid=` are declared and enforced: `subset=pid` clears lookup and readdir of non-process entries, `hidepid` gates pid dirs at NO_ACCESS on lookup and INVISIBLE on readdir through `has_pid_permissions`. Guest proof, one subset mount: x86 `sub_meminfo=0 sub_pids=59`, arm `sub_meminfo=0`, where before it was 1. | B1719 |
| FIXED B1718 | high | The trailing-symlink follow for `open(O_CREAT)` was a SECOND resolver living in the ABI shim: `pathresolve::resolve_parent_at_flags` re-entered the whole path walk once per link hop on a rewritten pathname, kept its own hop budget (`MAX_CREATE_LINK_HOPS`) beside the walk's `MAX_SYMLINK_DEPTH`, and probed the leaf with `vp.inode.lookup(name)` — an inode-level lookup that bypasses the dcache the rest of the walk resolves through. The reference follows the trailing component inside the SAME walk (`open_last_lookups` resolves the leaf, `step_into(WALK_TRAILING)` picks the link up and the walk continues on its target). Moved into the walker: a `LOOKUP_PARENT` walk with `follow` now resolves a LAST_NORM leaf through the same `lookup_child` every other component uses, and follows it through the same frame push, budget and `RESOLVE_*` gates. | `crates/kernel/vfs/src/namei/walk.rs` (leaf follow), `crates/kernel/vfs/src/namei/child.rs` (the shared resolution, extracted so there is one owner). `crates/kernel/vfs/tests/namei_create_trailing_link.rs` — 14 tests. Positive control: disabling only the follow decision turns 7 of 14 RED (`hop1` reports `(10,"hop1")` where the target is `(11,"stub")`), and the 4 no-follow cases stay green because they must be insensitive to it. | B1718 |
| FIXED B1718 | med | **8 tests in `syscalls/src/pathresolve/trailing_link.rs` had never executed once.** `pathresolve/mod.rs` is `#![cfg(target_os = "oxide-kernel")]`, so the module and its `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` compiled out entirely — `cargo test -p syscalls a_plain_create_follows_the_link` matched 0 tests in the whole package while reporting `ok`. The decision they covered now lives on `LookupFlags::set_open_create_trailing` in `vfs`, where the tests run, and is asserted against the real walker rather than in isolation. | Measured before the change: `cargo test -p syscalls -- trailing_link` → `running 0 tests`; by test name → 0 matched across every binary. After: the same rules are 3 of the 14 tests in `vfs::namei_create_trailing_link`, and `the_flag_rule_and_the_walk_agree_on_the_same_link` ties the flag to the walk's actual answer. | B1718 |
| FIXED C268 | high | **`make smoke-x86` FAILS on `origin/main` today — 3/3 attempts time out at 600 s — while the kernel it boots is healthy.** The gate greps for `Reached target basic.target`, and that line no longer reaches the serial console: a boot prints 7 other `Reached target` lines at t≈5 s (cryptsetup, getty, integritysetup, paths, remote-fs, slices, veritysetup) and then never that one. The kernel is fine — the same boot's sysrq task dump at t=554 s shows a full GNOME session scheduling normally (`gnome-software`, `fwupd`, `flatpak-system-helper`, `gsd-printer`, `systemd-userwork`), `Started systemd-journald.service`, and zero `File exists` errors — and systemd cannot reach `graphical.target` without passing `basic.target`. So the pre-push gate every kernel lane depends on is currently a guaranteed red, and a lane that trusts it will read its own change as a boot regression. This is the curated row that opens "The `boot-smoke` marker rides on systemd output, not the unconditional console path … it is fragile to exactly the quieting just done" — it has now actually broken. | Measured on this box, same command, same profile, back to back: detached worktree at `origin/main` (`0f39c79cb`) → attempts 1, 2, 3 all `timeout after 600s without login marker`, `exit=2`; branch `B1718` → identical shape on both the gnome and the `OXIDE_QUICKBOOT_PROFILE=lite` rootfs. `state.md` records this same gate as `PASS 66s` on `db658b904`, so it broke between that commit and `0f39c79cb`. Fix is a marker on the unconditional console path, not a systemd message. **Fixed by C268:** the gate no longer waits for a line the image decides whether to print — it types `echo OXIDE-AL"IVE"-OK` at the serial debug shell and matches the reply. The nonce is split by quotes in the command and whole only in the output, so the guest's echo of the typed bytes cannot match it and only a shell that EVALUATED the line can. Measured: x86 PASS 54s attempt 1, arm PASS 64s attempt 1, against 3/3 timeouts on the same box before. Positive control: a probe command whose output cannot match fails the run (exit=2). | C268 |
| FIXED B1702 + B1718 | high | `open(path, O_WRONLY\|O_CREAT\|O_TRUNC)` on a **dangling symlink whose parent directory exists** returns **EEXIST**. The reference follows the link and creates the target; EEXIST is only correct with `O_EXCL`. Minimal in-guest reproducer: `ln -sf /run/systemd/resolve/stub-resolv.conf /etc/rc2` (that directory exists, the file does not), then `: > /etc/rc2` → `sh: /etc/rc2: File exists`, and `python3 -c "import os;os.open('/etc/rc2',os.O_WRONLY\|os.O_CREAT\|os.O_TRUNC,0o644)"` → `FileExistsError: [Errno 17]`. This is why nothing in the guest can write `/etc/resolv.conf`, which ships as exactly such a dangling symlink — so it is on the path between a working IP stack and working name resolution. Needs its own lane: the fix sits in the `O_CREAT` open path and its errno ordering against `O_EXCL`/`O_NOFOLLOW` has to be audited against the reference. | See the archived open-flag truth table in `fixed-issues.md` (D473). **Closed in two parts.** The user-visible errno was fixed by `B1702` (`0aa0e2c97`), which made the create follow the trailing link. `B1718` then removed the reason it had been wrong: the follow was a SECOND resolver in the ABI shim that re-entered the whole walk per hop, kept its own symlink budget, and probed the leaf through the inode instead of the dcache. The follow now happens inside the walk, as `step_into(WALK_TRAILING)` does upstream. Verified by 14 tests over the real walker with a positive control that turns 7 of them red. | B1702, B1718 |
| FIXED C247 | low | `metadata/index.md` counters go stale and collide — index read `D 424` while `D432` was already merged. | `tools/next-branch.sh` derives the number from git refs + merge subjects and maxes against the table; `--check` fails when the table is behind, wired into `make ci` as `counter-check`. Positive control: the check reported `C247 already exists in git` before the table was bumped. | C247 |

### Net / socket

| FIXED 7d6992594 | med | Interface teardown closed and drained the IPv4 neighbour cache but left the retired generation's IPv6 neighbour cache open, so a retained handle could still expose stale NDP entries after unregister. | The common teardown now clears both family-generic neighbour caches before the generation drains. | FIXED F80901 | med | The `sendmmsg` differential covered only the `UIO_MAXIOV` limit. | `socket::send_batch` now exposes and tests its actual per-entry batch-flag decision; hosted cases cover interrupted first-entry propagation and completed-prefix return after a later restart.
